515 record kinds: 147 company-scoped, 19 install-wide, 349 needs-company. 276 routes: 98 company-scoped, 9 install-wide, 169 needs-company.

# Company inventory (R4, public Code / code/next)

All nine company-separation parts T1–T9 must pass before the pilot. This inventory is a migration baseline, not proof that Code already isolates every company.

Record kinds are groups of static entityType and partition-family identities (including canonical imported builder identities); associated identities share a row. Every scanned identity appears exactly once. Unproven/mixed families stay needs-company even when one caller is safe. This conservative classification deliberately does not infer safety from a name. Questions in those rows belong to T1.

The route total includes both dispatch patterns and the separately identified API Gateway route keys below. Prefix dispatch literals are included as patterns. Methods are hand-maintained: adding a METHOD on an existing path is NOT caught by this inventory. A checked=yes row proves only its explicitly named entry in COMPANY_CHECKED_ROUTES, and only with the optional membership reader enabled. Mixed-method or downstream-unproven rows remain needs-company. Core handleTaskRoute trusts its principal; other developer routes, admin, Slack and GitHub paths remain needs-company (T2).

Generic key/field forwarding rows are separately enumerated below and **excluded from the record-kind count**. Their file:line:column:field and exact expression form a ratchet: a new, removed, moved or changed site fails the test until reviewed. Imported constants and aliases use compiler symbols; unreadable constructors, unresolved sites and parse failures refuse the entire inventory. Source is read once under one total file/byte/item/node-visit budget (4,000 files, 40 MB, 100,000 identities/directory entries and 10 million traversal steps); the test runner retains its timeout; tests never read git history.

## Round 2 bounds and compatibility

Core workspace operations permit **10 reads**, each requesting at most 200 rows and limited by DynamoDB to a **1 MB page**, followed by one 1-row completion probe. Large rows can exhaust the read bound below 2,000 operations. The whole operation read refuses if the probe finds more; no partial operations are returned.

Checked task lists admit at most 2,000 items across SDK and task-action results per call. A result crossing that budget ends the page before the unfinished task; the exclusive-start cursor names the last fully examined index row. The crossing result is not used, still counts toward the 8 MB/512-command/10-second limits, and prevents further reads. A task too large to complete even on a fresh page still refuses with fixed words. Other limit failures remain refusals; shared workspace reads stay cached per call.

### Replaces

The prior blanket claim of byte-identical lists for implicit-company members with the check enabled is replaced by these exact exceptions, pinned in six filtered goldens (checked and legacy):
- Project/status filters: checked lists examine at most the requested `limit` index rows, instead of the legacy 200-row scan. A filtered-out row can produce an empty/short page with `nextCursor`; following it reaches the remaining tasks.
- Stale index timestamp: checked lists return the canonical task view's `updatedAt`, while legacy lists retain the index value unless status changes.
- Aggregate item bound: checked lists now return completed tasks plus continuation instead of the former 400 refusal when a later task crosses the budget. No task is skipped or partly returned.

The membership-absent path and the seven original golden pairs keep their response bytes. These exceptions do not enable the check or add company writers (T2/T3).

NEW DATA CLASS: short code quotes (at most 200 characters, redacted with redactText, rows only, never files) kept on claims and answer records for re-checking; never shown in the read routes. Notes, Mark-wrong reasons and answer records carry a person's words: the person erase deletes notes and answers and removes reasons (eraseWiki); answer records expire after 30 days, app-driven (a read treats an expired record as gone; a bounded expireAnswers sweep on every answer write deletes them; no table TTL attribute); the erase also renames the person as a note approver, build starter or flagger to former-member. Lead 4 J5 adds NO data class: the maintainers' gap list ("Questions the wiki could not answer") is read from the answer records (grouped by topic, counts different people, never names one; an erased or expired record takes its gap with it, tests/unit/answer-feedback.test.ts), and "Not right" is one boolean (`notRight`) on the asker's own answer record (it expires and is erased with the record). |

Web task channel C7 (core/notify/inbox-rows.ts): inboxKey is NOTIFY_INBOX#<companyId>#<developerId> / <session>#<kind>, written by queueAttention beside the pending row; the row also names its company and person. The reader (attentionPersonOf) builds the partition from the company it was asked about, ignores a row naming another company or id, and answers only a person who hashes to the id; the hosted address reader then requires that person's live membership in the same company. Expires 90 days after the last write. |

| record | entity:EVAL_PLAN | company-scoped | State table | no | - | DEC 253j(1) trusted eval plan (core/setup-agent/eval-spend.ts evalPlanKey): key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project) = orgScoped(companyId, SETUP_DRAFT#project), sort key EVAL_PLAN, next to EVAL_SPEND; written only by proposeEvalSpendApproval with the caller's checked company; readApprovedEvalPlan answers a row only when it names this company and project, its digest recomputes, and the approved EVAL_SPEND item of the same company names it. Two-company cases in tests/unit/eval-plan-source.test.ts (same project name in both companies, rows copied across partitions, re-labelled rows). |
| record | entity:SETUP_SNAPSHOT | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-DR (DEC 254z c) the setup snapshot saved by the setup approval (snapshot.ts setupSnapshotKey, sort key SNAPSHOT): paths and git object ids of setup files, never content; key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project) (core/setup-agent/keys.ts), the row names its company and project, and a row naming another company or project, an unknown row or one of another entity type reads as nothing (isRow in recheck-rows.ts for this row, and readSetupSnapshot in snapshot.ts for the snapshot row). Two-company and two-project cases, with the rows copied across partitions, in tests/unit/setup-drift-recheck.test.ts and setup-snapshot-approval.test.ts. |
| record | entity:SETUP_RECHECK | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-DR the free automatic setup re-check of one commit set (recheck-rows.ts recheckRowKey, sort key RECHECK#<key>): state, attempt, the check it runs, fixed change classes; expires with the cap's claims; key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project) (core/setup-agent/keys.ts), the row names its company and project, and a row naming another company or project, an unknown row or one of another entity type reads as nothing (isRow in recheck-rows.ts for this row, and readSetupSnapshot in snapshot.ts for the snapshot row). Two-company and two-project cases, with the rows copied across partitions, in tests/unit/setup-drift-recheck.test.ts and setup-snapshot-approval.test.ts. |

| record | entity:SETUP_REPAIR_NOTICE | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-DR the project's one admin card row when its setup is broken (repairNoticeKey, sort key REPAIR_NOTICE); shown only while the failed check is still the project's own setup state; key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project) (core/setup-agent/keys.ts), the row names its company and project, and a row naming another company or project, an unknown row or one of another entity type reads as nothing (isRow in recheck-rows.ts for this row, and readSetupSnapshot in snapshot.ts for the snapshot row). | record | entity:SETUP_REPAIR_TOLD | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-U5 the dedupe marker of the admins' "Setup needs repair" notice, one per re-check (repairToldKey in core/setup-agent/broken-notice.ts, sort key REPAIR_TOLD#<re-check key>): written first and only while absent (queueSetupBroken in core/notify/attention.ts), so one break is one notice per company, project and re-check, then expires after 62 days (upkeepExpiryOf); key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project), the row names its company and project, and the marker is only ever read by its own key. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/setup-cards-notices.test.ts. |
| record | builder:packages/broker/src/core/wiki-build/keys.ts:wikiBuildPartition | company-scoped | State table | no | - | Wiki W3 build run (core/wiki-build/keys.ts): one partition per company AND project, companyScoped(companyId, WIKI_BUILD#project), built only from the runtime's own company and a checked project name; every read (readWikiBuildPointer, storeCheckCache.get, the notes read) also requires the row's orgId and project to equal the caller's. Rows only, never files, so the company purge removes them by key. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/wiki-build-run.test.ts and tests/unit/wiki-build-plan-check.test.ts. |
| record | entity:WIKI_BUILD_RUN | company-scoped | State table | no | - | The project's build pointer (sk RUN): requester, limits, pinned commits, page plan, progress, spend. Wiki W3 build run (core/wiki-build/keys.ts): one partition per company AND project, companyScoped(companyId, WIKI_BUILD#project), built only from the runtime's own company and a checked project name; every read (readWikiBuildPointer, storeCheckCache.get, the notes read) also requires the row's orgId and project to equal the caller's. | record | entity:SYSTEM_RUNS_CONSENT | company-scoped | State table | no | - | The company's consent to Rovara's automatic runs (L4-P2, owner DEC 254zzt: effective = the operator ceiling AND this row). One row per company, `ORG#<company>#SYSTEM_RUNS` / `CONSENT` (core/system-runs/consent.ts: companyScoped, built only from a checked company id; every read requires the row's orgId to equal the runtime's company and `enabled` to be exactly true, anything else is no consent). Written only by a live company owner or admin in the setup PUT, in one transaction with the project's upkeep switch, or through the company's own route (L4-P3, core/system-runs/consent-control.ts: allowing and withdrawing need a live owner or admin, allowing only from the Rovara website's own client (DEC 254zzz91), no step-up), after an audit record; it keeps no person id. The company purge removes it by key. Two-company cases in tests/unit/system-runs-consent.test.ts, tests/unit/system-runs-company-control.test.ts and tests/unit/system-runs-hosted-gate.test.ts. |

| record | builder:packages/broker/src/core/upkeep/keys.ts:upkeepPartition | company-scoped | State table | no | - | Lead 4 N1 nightly upkeep (core/upkeep/keys.ts): one partition per company AND project, companyScoped(companyId, UPKEEP#project), built only from a checked company id and a checked project name; every read (readUpkeepSetting, the last-night read) also requires the row's orgId and project to equal the caller's. | record | entity:UPKEEP_SETTINGS | company-scoped | State table | no | - | The project's nightly upkeep switch (sk SETTINGS): enabled or not, when it changed, the request id of the change; absent or unreadable means OFF. Rows only, never files, no person's id or words (who switched upkeep is in the audit record), so the company purge removes them by key and the person erase has no step here. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/upkeep-night.test.ts and tests/unit/upkeep-notice.test.ts. |
| record | builder:packages/broker/src/core/system-runs/usage.ts:systemRunUsagePartition | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-P1 system runs (core/system-runs/usage.ts): one partition per company AND month, companyScoped(companyId, SYSTEM_RUN_USAGE#month), sort key project#request hash, built only from a checked company id and a checked project name; every read also requires the row's orgId, month and project to equal the caller's. Rows only: the company, project, kind and workspace of one system run (the machine time is measured from the workspace, never stored from a caller), no person's id or words, so the company purge removes them by key. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/system-starter.test.ts. |
| record | entity:SYSTEM_RUN_USAGE | company-scoped | State table | no | - | One row per system run (company, project, kind, workspace, request id, month); expires after 62 days. L4-P1 (core/system-runs/usage.ts): the company pays system runs' machine time, so the run is attributed to the company and project. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/system-starter.test.ts. |
| record | builder:packages/broker/src/core/system-runs/spend.ts:systemRunSpendPartition | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-SR1 nightly spend (core/system-runs/spend.ts): one partition per company AND month, companyScoped(companyId, SYSTEM_RUN_SPEND#month), sort key project#request hash, built only from a checked company id and a checked project name; every read also requires the row's orgId, project, month and kind to equal the caller's. Rows only: the company, project, kind, task and end of one finished AI system run and its cost in micro-dollars ONLY when known (never 0 for unknown); no person's id or words, so the company purge removes them by key. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/system-runs-nightly-wiki.test.ts. |
| record | entity:SYSTEM_RUN_SPEND | company-scoped | State table | no | - | One row per finished nightly AI run (company, project, kind wiki_refresh, task, request id, month, how it ended, cost when known); expires after 62 days. L4-SR1 (core/system-runs/spend.ts): the company's spend line for nightly upkeep, attributed to the project. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/system-runs-nightly-wiki.test.ts. |
| record | entity:UPKEEP_PASS_CLAIM | company-scoped | State table | no | - | The claim of one project's upkeep night for one UTC day (sk PASS#day), written only while absent so a second fire of the hourly pass starts nothing; given back when the night could not start; expires after 62 days. L4-P1 (core/system-runs/night-pass.ts) in the project's upkeep partition, companyScoped(companyId, UPKEEP#project). Two-company case in tests/unit/upkeep-night-pass.test.ts. |
| record | entity:UPKEEP_LAST | company-scoped | State table | no | - | The last upkeep night (sk LAST): its day, a hash of the default-branch commits it saw (so a night with nothing new skips the free checks) and its fixed outcome word; expires after 62 days. Lead 4 N1 nightly upkeep (core/upkeep/keys.ts): one partition per company AND project, companyScoped(companyId, UPKEEP#project), built only from a checked company id and a checked project name; every read (readUpkeepSetting, the last-night read) also requires the row's orgId and project to equal the caller's. Rows only, never files, no person's id or words (who switched upkeep is in the audit record), so the company purge removes them by key and the person erase has no step here. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/upkeep-night.test.ts and tests/unit/upkeep-notice.test.ts. |
| record | entity:UPKEEP_NIGHT | company-scoped | State table | no | - | One row per decided upkeep night (sk NIGHT#day): fixed outcome words only; expires after 62 days. Lead 4 N1 nightly upkeep (core/upkeep/keys.ts): one partition per company AND project, companyScoped(companyId, UPKEEP#project), built only from a checked company id and a checked project name; every read (readUpkeepSetting, the last-night read) also requires the row's orgId and project to equal the caller's. Rows only, never files, no person's id or words (who switched upkeep is in the audit record), so the company purge removes them by key and the person erase has no step here. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/upkeep-night.test.ts and tests/unit/upkeep-notice.test.ts. |
| record | entity:UPKEEP_NOTICE | company-scoped | State table | no | - | The dedupe marker of the admins' notice of an upkeep pause (sk NOTICE#month#reason): written first and only while absent, so one pause is one notice per company, project, month and reason; expires after 62 days. Lead 4 N1 nightly upkeep (core/upkeep/keys.ts): one partition per company AND project, companyScoped(companyId, UPKEEP#project), built only from a checked company id and a checked project name; every read (readUpkeepSetting, the last-night read) also requires the row's orgId and project to equal the caller's. Rows only, never files, no person's id or words (who switched upkeep is in the audit record), so the company purge removes them by key and the person erase has no step here. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/upkeep-night.test.ts and tests/unit/upkeep-notice.test.ts. |
| record | builder:packages/broker/src/core/ci-evidence/eval-check-setting.ts:evalCheckPartition | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-EVCI2 the setup card's eval check from the customer's CI (core/ci-evidence/eval-check-setting.ts): one partition per company AND project, companyScoped(companyId, CIEVAL#project), built only from a checked company id and a checked project name; every read (readEvalCheckSetting) also requires the row's orgId and project to equal the caller's. Rows only, never files, no person's id or words (who confirmed it is in the audit record), so the company purge removes them by key and the person erase has no step here. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/eval-check-routes.test.ts and tests/unit/eval-check-end-to-end.test.ts. |
| record | entity:CI_EVAL_CHECK | company-scoped | State table | no | - | The project's confirmed eval check (sk CHECK): the check's name and reporting app (or none), when it changed, the request id of the change; durable (no expiry); absent, unreadable or another company's or project's row reads as nothing named. Written only by the setup card's route (core-host/eval-check-routes.ts, local/app/eval-check.ts) for a live company owner or admin or the project's administrator, after an audit record, and only for a check the project's own workflow files proposed. Rows only, never files, no person's id or words (who confirmed it is in the audit record), so the company purge removes them by key and the person erase has no step here. Two-company and two-project cases in tests/unit/eval-check-routes.test.ts and tests/unit/eval-check-end-to-end.test.ts. |
| record | entity:EVAL_DISCOVERY | company-scoped | State table | no | - | DEC 253n(1) setup eval discovery report (core/setup-agent/eval-discovery.ts evalDiscoveryKey): key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project) with sort key EVAL_DISCOVERY#<task>; written only by the setup-agent callback for a running setup run of the callback's own company (setupRunOfOperation), put-if-absent, with the commits the broker recorded; the confirm route (POST /v1/app/projects/{p}/setup-agent/eval-confirm) reads it only at the caller's own company's key and refuses a row whose company, project, task or recomputed digest differ. Two-company cases in tests/unit/eval-discovery-confirm.test.ts (B naming A's run, A's row copied into B's partition, re-labelled rows). |
| record | entity:EVAL_DEPENDENCY_LIST | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-E12 (DEC 254zzz55 N6) the free dry run's dependency list (core/setup-agent/eval-dependency-list.ts evalDependencyListKey): key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project) with sort key EVAL_DEPENDENCY_LIST (one row per project, the newest setup run's list replaces the older); written only by the setup-agent callback (body kind `eval_dry_run`) for a running setup run of the callback's own company (setupRunOfOperation); the list is the contract's fixed shape (databases by port, outside host names, variable names, `modelCalls` exactly 0; no log line, value or path); read only at the caller's own company's key and a row whose company or project differs, or whose list is no longer the contract's shape, reads as absent. Two-company cases in tests/unit/eval-dependency-list.test.ts. |
| record | entity:EVAL_HEALTH_REQUEST | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-EVC (DEC 254zzz20 D5) the opt-in "Check my evals now" yes (core/setup-agent/eval-health-request.ts evalHealthRequestKey): key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project) with sort key EVAL_HEALTH_REQUEST; written only by requestEvalHealthCheck for a caller of the key's own company who holds a live owner/admin/maintainer role and only under an approved spend item; read only while it names this company and project and the approval it was made under still stands. Two-company cases in tests/unit/eval-setup-card.test.ts and tests/unit/core-host-eval-card-routes.test.ts. |
| record | entity:EVAL_WRAPPER | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-EVW4 (DEC 254zzz43) the eval wrapper pull request a PERSON asked Rovara to open (core/setup-agent/eval-wrapper.ts keyOf): key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project) with sort key EVAL_WRAPPER#<id> (id = hash of project, repository place and wrapper language); written only by the card's POST route for the caller's own company (openEvalWrapperPullRequest: never by the setup callback), claimed put-if-absent or against the state and update time it read; every read goes through the caller's company key and a row whose company or project differ is ignored. The row names the person (by) and the audit names them too. Two-company cases in tests/unit/eval-wrapper.test.ts, tests/unit/eval-wrapper-setup.test.ts and tests/unit/core-host-eval-wrapper-routes.test.ts. |
| record | entity:EVAL_WRAPPER_FILE | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-EVW4 (DEC 254zzz43) what the last check found at the wrapper file's fixed path on the project's DEFAULT branch, and the test run that follows it (core/setup-agent/eval-wrapper.ts fileKeyOf): key is the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project) with sort key EVAL_WRAPPER_FILE#<id> (id = hash of project, repository place, wrapper language and the eval's selection: one row per eval); written only by detectEvalWrappers (a read of the code host through a guarded target, for the caller's own company) and recordEvalWrapperTest; no file text and no person is kept (present/absent/other, same/edited/outdated, a digest of the text, and the test state). Every read goes through the caller's company key and a row whose company or project differ is ignored. Two-company cases in tests/unit/eval-wrapper.test.ts and tests/unit/eval-wrapper-setup.test.ts. |
| record | entity:EVAL_WRAPPER_DISMISSED | company-scoped | State table | no | - | L4-EVW4 (DEC 254zzz43) the project's "Not now" for the eval wrapper card (core/setup-agent/eval-wrapper.ts dismissedKeyOf): one row at sort key EVAL_WRAPPER_DISMISSED in the company-scoped setup partition setupPartition(companyId, project); written only by the card's POST route (a person's act, audited naming them) and removed by "Set up now"; it holds the setup run's report digest it was set aside for (a newer report asks again) and the person's id. Every read goes through the caller's company key and a row whose company or project differ is ignored. Two-company cases in tests/unit/eval-wrapper.test.ts and tests/unit/core-host-eval-wrapper-routes.test.ts. |

| record | entity:TASK_INVOLVED | company-scoped | State table | no | - | Web task channel C5: one person's involved-task pointers (ORG#<company>#INVOLVED#<person> / TASK#<createdAt>#<taskId>), written by C4 when a person is added to a task and deleted when denied. Pointer only (ids and times), never task content. Read only by listInvolvedTasks, which asks the flow's own company and the caller's live id for the partition, skips any row whose company, person, kind, key or task creation time disagrees, and re-checks every task against its live company and access before showing it. Two-company, forged-row and stale-row tests: task-flow-involved.test.ts. |

| record | entity:BROWSER_EVIDENCE_UPLOAD | needs-company | State table | no | - | TE-H1 slice 2 (DEC 241b): one upload row per CHECKS operation under WORKSPACE#<workspace>, like the other chunked uploads. The callback reads the operation from the company-scoped store and refuses any operation the broker did not send the recipe to; the keeper reads the row back only for the same workspace and operation; the pictures reach the company-partitioned evidence store. T1: the row key itself is not company-scoped. |
| record | entity:BROWSER_EVIDENCE_RECIPE | company-scoped | State table | no | - | TE-H1 slice 3 (owner decision DEC 241c): the project's browser evidence recipe, one row per project, under `projectBrowserEvidenceRecipeKey(orgId, project)` (the company's own project partition). Every read names the company and project and checks both again on the row (`readStoredRecipe`: another company's, another project's or a damaged row is no recipe), every write is conditional on the version the page saw, and a sign-in's reference must be the project's own. The row holds no secret: a sign-in is a form and a NAME; its values live in the company's own secret path. |

## GitHub installation operator runbook (T5)

Only an install operator with existing direct broker Lambda invocation permission may run these events. They have no HTTP route; company-admin tokens and HTTP-shaped invocations cannot claim. Keep the optional binding and membership dependencies behind the staging/review gate. No IAM or infrastructure change is included here.

Before backfill, inspect the GitHub App's installations and confirm which belong to the implicit company. Freeze installation changes while walking pages. **Bind the pilot company's installation with `agentx.github-binding-claim`** before backfill if it already exists, or after backfill if it is installed later. Backfill preserves explicit bindings and assigns only validated unbound installations to the implicit company. It cannot infer a company's ownership from a GitHub account name.

An operator invokes the existing broker Lambda with this payload (substitute verified positive installation id and company id):

```json
{"source":"agentx.github-binding-claim","installationId":77,"companyId":"org_aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa"}
```

The broker verifies the installation through this App's GitHub credentials, then atomically writes its conditional binding and `AUDIT#CLAIM` record. A second claim, including the same company, is refused. Unknown, foreign, invalid and already-bound requests have the same fixed refusal. `boundBy`/audit actor is `operator-lambda`; Retain the operator invocation evidence to identify the IAM caller. Never try to reassign an existing binding with a raw table write. Later T3/Cloud self-service must verify WITH GITHUB that the person administers the installation account.

Next invoke `{"source":"agentx.github-binding-backfill"}`. Each call makes one installation-list request (25 rows, 1 MB response, 5-second HTTP timeout), no repository/token fan-out, then at most 25 validated binding writes under a fresh 304-command/1 MB/10-second store budget. On `more:true`, invoke again with the returned numeric `continuation`. A persisted page checkpoint refuses skipped pages. A full page requires a following page; only the final page seals the completion marker. On a failure, retry the same page; inspect the saved progress if a successful response was lost. Confirm `more:false`, the marker, implicit bindings and the pilot binding before enabling any second company.

Recovery similarly returns `more:true` and an opaque `continuation` when bounded listing stops. Invoke `agentx.github-webhook-recovery` again with that exact continuation until `more` is absent. Each call lists at most 50 companies and processes at most 100 deliveries (25 rows per company page). Registry/partition reads alone spend the shared 512-command/2,000-item/8 MB/10-second budget; processing uses the plain client and existing expiring delivery leases. Listing stops before claiming any unfinished page. Invalid legacy rows are skipped and counted in `skipped`; inspect and repair them under the owning company's authority. Other company partitions still run.

Bindings absent retains the existing webhook/recovery response bytes. With bindings present and membership absent, retry fails closed. Legacy administrator registration/retry identities without a checked developer identity also fail closed; the authenticated client creation path supplies its checked developer id.

The new member-request forwarding site is `packages/broker/src/aws/company-member-forwarding.ts:companyMembersThroughLambda` (company-scoped). Only revoke, role-set and list are admitted by a strict schema after web authentication. Lane W can select the fixed list/revoke/setRole methods from companyMemberActionsThroughLambda without constructing a kind outside this module. Company and actor come from a strongly read, live admin/owner membership; mismatched requested companies and extra claims refuse before invoke. Member writes transactionally recheck supplied actors. A self-actor uses the target snapshot condition, and an actor who is also the owner witness uses that stronger owner ConditionCheck (DynamoDB forbids duplicate item actions). This replaces the old operator-only revoke pin; restore and all other operator-only kinds remain forbidden in broker forwarding. No HTTP route or Gateway path is added. The member mirror only locates an owner candidate; last-owner protection checks that candidate's authoritative membership in the mutation transaction. Existing person-global authoritative membership stays the R4 pinned exception. IAM and complete mirror backfill are prerequisites to enabling this read path; no GSI or infrastructure edit is included.

## Merge-up: spec 065 and new task routes

Compared with the original R4 baseline, the incoming developer-routes helpers are projectsAPersonUses, githubFullName, readClientProject, authenticateClientToken, routeClientRequest and routeLinkingRequest; all are retained. The access check now resolves membership for both client authentication forms and filters both task grants and administrator projects. Client detail checks the final revision again before returning repositories. New GET messages/comments/diff pass resolved membership into the existing downstream checks. With companies absent, the incoming code/next response bytes remain unchanged; the client routes are always on since DEC 254zzz81 (the clientRoutes opt-in is gone).

The nine new dispatch patterns and four new record families are listed above. No new gateway path was added. Existing sign-in token/authorize routes still serve install identity; sign-in client registration, its scopes/keepSecret update, and per-company ownership remain T3 (entity:SIGN_IN_CLIENT and /v1/admin/sign-in-clients). routeLinkingRequest reuses the same checked access/detail paths and retains the existing self-owned channel-link paths. Token authentication itself is unchanged.

Named behavioural gaps in tests/contract/company-merge-up.test.ts: T2/T3 client member GET/PUT/DELETE operate across company boundaries; T2 task POST messages/comments do not resolve membership. These routes need shared writer/registry/provider changes beyond developer-routes.ts. GET diff's task gate is checked, but its workspace pull-request provider still lacks company binding (T2/T7); enabling a repository provider requires that follow-up. None of these gaps is reported as isolation PASS.

T5 deployment gate: installation claims are install-operator-only. Before enabling a second company, the controller must complete the required staging GitHub installation backfill and verify its marker. The later T3/Cloud self-serve connect flow must verify with GitHub that the claimant administers the installation account; never trust on first use. T5 now wires the spec 065 listing/create checks and operator-invoked backfill event. Backfill now reads validated installation-only pages of 25 with durable continuation; see the operator runbook below. Production dependencies remain absent until controller activation after staging proof and review. All nine company-separation parts must pass first.

Forwarding sites were re-anchored to the merged sources without weakening the scanner. The sole new expression is conversationSubject(conversation) in the LAST_EVENT sort key; it preserves conversation identity but requires T4 company binding. Record and route totals are recomputed by the existing ratchet, which reads no git history.

## Merge-up 2: full-access callers, chosen names and task readers

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| record | builder:packages/broker/src/e2e/legacy-key-scope.ts:scopedProjectPk | needs-company | State table | no | - | T1: mixed scoped/legacy constructor accepts a legacy-unscoped selector without resolving membership; named known-gap test pins that responsibility. Incoming e2e-legacy-scope-fallback tests prove existing callers restrict legacy reads to the implicit company. |
| route | /v1/dev/me/name | install-wide | sign-in table | no | PUT | Verified person's own sign-in identity/display name, deliberately shared across their company contexts; developer-own-name tests cover caller-only updates. |
| route | /v1/dev/tasks/{taskId}/readers | needs-company | State table | yes | GET | Membership-resolved task gate, including viewer fallback, under the total read budget. Returned person/project-grant metadata is still install-wide; T2/T3 recipient membership binding is a named known gap. |
| route | /v1/dev/tasks/{taskId}/readers/{id} | needs-company | State table | no | PUT, DELETE | T2/T3: reader writes require company-aware recipient lookup and write/replay fences; named PUT/DELETE known-gap tests. |

The three conflict regions retain upstream nameChosen/displayNameChosen, administrator grants and precedence, and authenticateCaller for full-access clients, together with R4 listProjects(companyId) and every merge-up 1 check. The linking dispatcher's new project catalog now resolves membership with the same bounded company reads as the native catalog. Full-access credentials go through these same checks.

The new loadReadableTask viewer fallback rechecks recordInCompany before consulting a reader grant, so it cannot undo an owner's company refusal. GET events and GET readers resolve membership at dispatch. Reader-grant writes remain a T2/T3 follow-up; the new linking workspace catalog retains the existing catalog's known company gap. Install-wide own-name updates are deliberately not company data. SIGN_IN_CLIENT.fullAccess and DEVELOPER.displayNameChosen extend existing records, not new families. The DEVTASK row explicitly includes READER sort records; the scanner groups these under the existing partition family.

All incoming source identities are classified without changing the scanner; forwarding expressions are identical and only source locations moved. No new API Gateway path appeared. The inventory's method column remains manual, and optional-company read checks do not claim isolation for unchecked writers, recipient metadata or workspace providers.

## T6 secret records and entry points

T6 uses R4's `CompanyMembershipReader` from `packages/broker/src/aws/company-check.ts`. Each bound operation resolves membership afresh; missing, malformed or throwing membership refuses with the existing fixed sentence. A valid foreign connector, hook, MCP or test-account name reads as missing before secret-client selection or any Secrets Manager call. The adapter's absent-options byte goldens and R4's absent-`deps.companies` route goldens remain the compatibility controls. This type-only reader swap changes no runtime guard or default.

The counted rows above retain **needs-company** for mixed or incompletely wired record/route families. The rows below classify secret storage and entry points outside the scanner's record/dispatch grammar; they are **not extra counted identities**. A company-scoped secret adapter does not establish end-to-end route isolation. Local routes are outside the scanner's `aws/` dispatch set; contract-only hook paths and worker references must not be presented as scanned, checked routes.

| Surface / record kind | Class | Storage / route | Evidence and remaining owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Connector secret values | company-scoped | other: `agentx/<env>/org-connectors/<company>/<id>` | `company-secrets.ts`, `secrets-manager/index.ts`, `connection-secrets.ts`; `secrets-company-member`, `aws-secrets-manager-company`, `secrets-tagged-session` tests. Membership-bound composition is opt-in; R2 wires hosted callers. |
| Connection metadata and directory | needs-company | State: `ORG_CONNECTION`, `ORG_CONNECTION_DIRECTORY`, `connectionKey`, `connectionsKey` (counted above) | Keys include company; records store only presence/version. `connections-store.test.ts` covers versions and cross-company refusal. Caller membership is still an R2/T2 obligation. |
| Hook secret values | company-scoped | other: `agentx/<env>/org-hooks/<company>/<id>` | Same T6 reader/writer guards; create uses `rovara:use=hook`. `secrets-company-names`, `aws-secrets-manager-company`, `secrets-tagged-session` tests. |
| Hook record, directory, replay and rate; hook lookup | needs-company | State: contract constructors `hookKey`, `hookDirectoryKey`, `hookEventKey`, `hookRateKey`; global `hookLookupKey` | `contracts/src/event-hooks.ts` scopes the first four; lookup is deliberately global before signature verification. No corresponding hook dispatcher/store is present in this Code tree. T2/T8 must prove signature, lookup, replay and membership authority; T6 does not promote them. |
| Registered MCP token / stdio secret document | company-scoped | other: `orgMcpServerSecretName` uses `org-connectors/<company>/<serverId>` | `context-sources/wiring.ts` uses the connection secret port; T6 name and adapter tests include this shared family. Hosted membership wiring remains R2. |
| Registered MCP metadata | needs-company | State: `orgScoped(orgId, "MCPSRV")` / `SRV#<serverId>` (partition builder counted above) | `context-sources/mcp-servers.ts` scopes keys; local caller authority alone does not prove all callers. R2/T2 integration remains. |
| Test-account secret values | company-scoped | other: `agentx/<env>/org-test-accounts/<company>/<account>` | `contracts/src/test-account.ts` builds names/tags; T6 reader same-answer tests cover this family. T6 connection writer accepts connector/hook names only. T7 owns worker dynamic-reference authorization; this lane does not supply a test-account writer. |
| Account definitions / latest pointers | needs-company | State: `E2E_ACCOUNT`, `E2E_ACCOUNT_LATEST` (counted above) | Company/project keys hold references; T7 must bind worker execution and secret resolution to the approved account. |
| Connector execution and discovery | needs-company | POST `/v1/workspaces/{id}/connectors/{id}/call`; GET `/v1/workspaces/{id}/connectors/{id}/tools` (counted above, checked=no) | T6 helpers do not add `deps.companies` at these routes; R2/T2/T7 retain wiring and execution proof. |
| Local connection and MCP reads / page writes | needs-company | GET `/v1/dev/connections`, GET `/v1/dev/connections/{id}`, GET `/v1/dev/mcp-servers`; owner page actions | `local/app/connections.ts` compares the caller principal with the install company and gates writes on a fresh page. It does not resolve R4 membership; other HTTP methods refuse. R2/T2 multi-company binding remains; these local paths are outside the scanned AWS route set. |
| Hook delivery | needs-company | Contract-only POST `/v1/hooks/{source}/{hookId}` | Described by `contracts/src/event-hooks.ts`; no matching Code dispatcher in this tree. T2/T8 must prove delivery authority, with T6 secret guard as one layer. |
| Test-account dynamic reference | needs-company | Worker `test-account-secret.ts`, no dedicated scanned secret HTTP route | T7 owns worker authority and role cutover; T6 cannot claim the worker path is membership-checked. |
| GitHub App key/webhook and Slack install secrets | install-wide | other: configured install secret names/ARNs | T4/T5; outside T6 company-name families. |
| Model provider keys | install-wide | other: model-key port and model-runtime environment ARNs | lane G; no model-key family is admitted by T6. |

Old unscoped names belong only to the explicitly configured implicit company in hosted mode; `companyPaths` without an owner refuses them. GitHub App/Slack install secrets remain T4/T5; model keys remain lane G. Real IAM/TagSession policies, backfill, role split and staging evidence remain in `T6-SECRETS-IAM-20261114-v2`; fake tests do not establish deployed IAM behavior. No checked=yes row is added by T6.

## Merge-up 3: proof holds and mark-ready overrides

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| record | entity:PROOF_HOLD | needs-company | State table | no | - | T1: proofHoldKey constructs DEVTASK/task + PROOF_HOLD/repository/number without company. holdRecordOf/readProofHold check orgId and taskId, but judgeProofHold compares the raw stateKey and writes without a company fence: a same-task collision can suppress or replace another company's judgement. Named known-gap test in company-inventory.test.ts. |
| record | entity:PROOF_OVERRIDE | needs-company | State table | no | - | T1: proofOverrideKey constructs DEVTASK/task + PROOF_OVERRIDE/repository/number without company. overrideRecordOf and markReadyAnyway check company/task and live roles; their absent/head conditions do not namespace uniqueness by company. A foreign row at that key blocks an otherwise authorized mark-ready. Named known-gap test in company-inventory.test.ts; foreign override contents are refused. |
| record | entity:PROOF_HOLD_HISTORY | company-scoped | State table | no | - | historyKey uses companyScoped(orgId, "PROOF_HOLD_HISTORY") and validates the project sort key; every history reader/writer uses that constructor. The same-project two-company behavioural test in company-inventory.test.ts proves separate history totals. |
| record | entity:CI_CHECK_TIMES | company-scoped | State table | no | - | TE-E0 A3: checkTimesKey uses companyScoped(orgId, "CI_CHECK_TIMES") with a project#repository sort key; readCheckTimes also refuses an item whose orgId is not the caller's, and recordCheckTimes writes only through that constructor. Informational (learned wait times), never a verdict. |
| record | entity:ROVARA_CHECK | company-scoped | State table | no | - | TE-B4: summaryCheckKey uses companyScoped(orgId, "ROVARA_CHECK") with the task, repository and number as the sort key; syncSummaryCheck and stopSummaryChecks only read and write through it, and summaryCheckRecordOf refuses a row of another company or task. The two-company test in company-inventory.test.ts proves separate rows for the same task key. |
| record | entity:FULLRUN | company-scoped | State table | no | - | TE-C3: the full-suite follow-up, its task pointer and the day count all live under companyScoped(orgId, "FULLRUN") (runKey, latestKey, dayKey), with project and task as validated sort parts; readFollowup and every transition also require the stored orgId to match. The two-company tests in core-full-suite-followup.test.ts prove a separate daily cap and that another company can neither read nor answer a follow-up. |

proofHoldKey and proofOverrideKey reuse the existing pk:DEVTASK family; historyKey reuses builder:packages/contracts/src/ports/company-scope.ts:companyScoped. Those shared/mixed families retain needs-company. The three new entity identities above count as three record rows. The validated project sort component is generic forwarding within the history partition and has its own forwarding row; it contributes no record kind. Two existing merge.ts forwarding sites moved one line with identical expressions. No route or gateway identity changed. Existing company checks and canDecide are retained; these classifications do not claim full isolation for the unscoped task-key writers.

| record | entity:COMPANY | company-scoped | sign-in table | no | - | T3 companyKey uses companyScoped(companyId, "COMPANY"); only operator creation writes the metadata. DEC 254b (b): the operator's company import (developer/company-import.ts) writes it too, attribute_not_exists, createdBy "import" with importedAt; its undo deletes only that unchanged record, once no member is left. |
| record | entity:COMPANY_PREAPPROVAL | company-scoped | sign-in table | no | - | T3 preapprovalKey/preapprovalHashKey use companyScoped(companyId, "PREAPPROVAL"); joining consumes only the method company's live, unexpired approval atomically. INVITE-RESEND updates only a live, unexpired pending row, conditionally replacing its inviteHash and expiry in the same transaction as old-link withdrawal, new-link creation and audit. |
| record | entity:COMPANY_INVITE_LINK | company-scoped | sign-in table | no | - | DEC 254h: inviteLinkKey uses companyScoped(companyId, "INVITE_LINK"), keyed by the link secret's SHA-256 only; bound to one pre-approval of the same company (which names the hash), spent with it and the membership in one transaction. INVITE-RESEND writes the fresh link and withdraws the old link atomically with the preapproval update and company audit. |
| record | entity:COMPANY_LOG | company-scoped | sign-in table | no | - | T3 operatorWrite appends an id-only log under companyScoped(companyId, "COMPANY_LOG") in the mutation transaction, including the INVITE-RESEND event. |

A code belongs to no company until one person spends it in the transaction that makes their company; it then names that company. |
| record | entity:COMPANY_INVITE_LOG; pk:COMPANY_INVITE_LOG | install-wide | sign-in table | no | - | DEC 254a: install audit row per invite create, revoke and use and per self-serve switch change, in the same transaction; code ids and opaque person/company ids only. |
| record | entity:COMPANY_SELF_SERVE_SETTING; pk:COMPANY_SELF_SERVE | install-wide | sign-in table | no | - | DEC 254a: the operator's self-serve switch (absent = open); rechecked inside the create transaction. |
| record | pk:COMPANY_SELF_CREATE_RATE | install-wide | sign-in table | no | - | DEC 253z: per-person, per-hour try counter for self-serve create, TTL expiresAt; no company data. |

## T3 independent Slice A work (company mode remains off)

T3 supplies membership, preapprovals, company issuers, scoped email and per-company clients behind the opt-in switch. Registry mutations serialize through the approved revision CAS; operator install-level clients are explicit exceptions. The production broker injects membership into checked routes, admin actions and client planners. Invite routes accept only member approvals from membership admins/owners. T4 Slack, T2 operator/admin separation and PROJECT_MEMBERS remain named gaps. This inventory does not claim pilot readiness.

The join performs at most four consistent item reads (including its single conditional-race reread), each bounded to DynamoDB's 400 KB item ceiling: at most 1,638,400 bytes and four items in total. It performs at most one join transaction and no pagination. Preapproval listing has one 100-item query, a 1.1 MB total response cap and a 10-second elapsed check; the host's existing 15-second Lambda timeout remains the outer I/O guard. More rows are explicitly reported with `more: true`. Company providers, secrets and JWKS caches each hold at most 50 entries; no membership cache is introduced.

Retained gaps: second-company Slack binding and directory (T4); operator/company-admin authority split (T2); PROJECT_MEMBERS and project-name grant isolation (T1/T2); named scopes (#646). Step-up and passkeys were removed (owner DEC 254zzz73). No inventory scanner or checked-route ratchet is widened.

| route | /v1/dev/company | install-wide | sign-in table | no | POST | DEC 253z/254a: self-serve create by a signed-in person with no company, web app only; the new company comes from the sign-in Lambda's transaction, never the request. Every other company route refuses a session with no company. |
| route | /v1/admin/company-invites | install-wide | sign-in table | no | GET | DEC 254a: install administrator claim; code ids, status and times only. |
| route | /v1/admin/company-import | install-wide | State table | no | GET | DEC 254b (b): install administrator claim; reads only. The preview of copying one company of the install's old layout (State table ORG#<company>) into Code's company records (sign-in table), or of its undo: opaque person ids, roles and fixed reasons, never a name or an email. The company id is the request's, validated; every read is that company's own partition or a person's own record. |
| route | /v1/answers | company-scoped | State table | no | POST | L4-D79 (DEC 254zzz79 (3)): not a route this broker serves but the answers-route suffix of the company's model gateway (`<gateway>/v1/answers`, the base an Anthropic client adds `/v1/messages` to), built in packages/broker/src/aws/slack-model-access.ts:19 (const) and :55 (baseUrl) for the Slack service's `POST /v1/service/model-access`. Company-scoped: the company is the Slack workspace's own binding read live (`companyOf(thread.teamId)`, never a caller, header or body; the install team's implicit company and an unbound workspace are refused), the token minted for it names that company only (core-host/gateway-answers.ts mintAnswersCallToken, answers step, no person), and the gateway meters the call in that company's answers month and combined cap on the company's own AI source. Nothing is read or written in the State table for this path. |
| route | /v1/dev/company/preapprovals | company-scoped | sign-in table | yes | POST | T3: CLI token, live membership admin/owner; member-only body; company and actor derived from membership. |
| route | /v1/dev/company/preapprovals/{id}/resend | company-scoped | sign-in table | yes | POST | INVITE-RESEND (DEC 254zzz184): live company owner/admin via the website client; only a pending invite; atomically replaces the single-use link and appends the company audit row. |
| route | /v1/dev/company/preapprovals/ | company-scoped | sign-in table | yes | DELETE | T3: prefix dispatch validates exact hash path, live membership admin/owner and scoped withdrawal. |
| route | /v1/dev/company/preapprovals/{id} | company-scoped | sign-in table | yes | DELETE | T3: runtime hash validation; foreign and missing approvals return identical not-found bytes. |

## Lane W part 4: company member routes (company-member-routes.ts)
Every route below is `full` in the route table (DEC ANSWER-4), re-reads the caller's live membership from the sign-in table (company must equal resolveCompany's, not revoked, admin or owner; a plain member gets not found), and reaches member data only through T3b's companyMemberActionsThroughLambda, which re-checks that membership and builds the identity request itself. A target of another company, a missing one and an id that does not read answer the same "member not found". Two-company, owner-only and mutant cases are in tests/contract/company-member-routes.test.ts.
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| route | /v1/dev/company/members | company-scoped | sign-in table | yes | GET | Page of the membership company's members (T3b list); a cursor another company's list gave is refused before forwarding; names/emails read per listed person id only. |
| route | /v1/dev/company/members/{id}/removal-preview | company-scoped | State table | yes | GET | Target membership must name the caller's company; tasks counted only when the task record is in that company and owned by the target; projects only when their latest revision is in that company; bounded, past a bound the whole preview is refused. |

### S3 catalog migration (PR A+B)
PROJECT_CATALOG_INDEX is an OrgSettingName at orgSettingsKey(company, PROJECT_CATALOG_INDEX), under the existing SETTINGS family. It is read only for the implicit company (S3 and forward-write D1); verify --mark refuses a named company, whose partition is authoritative without it. Its verifiedAt/count/runId are written only by controller verify --mark --apply after the history/catalog comparison (partition-only own rows are reported as extra, not refused); it has no separate entityType. Company purge removes the flag and catalog. A backfillRunId tags only newly created core copies/catalog rows; backfillMarked on the catalog records that this company has been marked and prevents undo after unmark. The legacy entity:PROJECT_CATALOG line remains needs-company until PR C.

Future move contract (not implemented): one transaction ConditionChecks legacy companyId, deletes the old company catalog row fenced on latestRevision, and puts the new company row with attribute_not_exists. Cross-company takeover remains refused. Core/local-only writers retain their own company-keyed model; they are inventoried separately from AWS dual writers.

### C-PC company concurrency fields

Wiki builds reserve at most two token-owned `wikiBuildSlot0` / `wikiBuildSlot1` fields on the existing company-scoped `orgLimitKey(companyId)` workspace counter. Each field holds a project name, a random ownership token and an expiry matching W3's existing stale-run deadline (wall clock plus 60 minutes). The workspace `count` is untouched. The existing `WIKI_BUILD_RUN` pointer carries the matching field/token for conditional release on end; failed starts release their reservation. No new record family, code text or person-authored text is added. Company purge already covers both keys. Tests: `tests/unit/wiki-company-cap.test.ts` (two projects, two companies, failed starts, stale recovery and delayed ends). Free-map admission follows W0's in-process cache lifetime: two computations per company across projects and service instances; joined requests and cached maps take no slot.
L4-B5 (task log read route and chat mirror): `local/app/app.ts` gained one import line, so its two pinned sites (the line-394 query row and the workspace-key forwarding row) moved down by one line (395 and 417) and are unchanged otherwise. No record kind, key constructor, forwarding site or route family was added: the log read reuses the `TASKLOG#` rows of the task's own partition through `readTaskLog` (this company's rows of this task only) and the existing `CHANNEL_NOTICE#` claim rows under a new `log#` sort key.

L4-EVS2 (the eval-plan answer's environment): `local/app/app.ts` gained one import line, so its two pinned sites (the query row and the workspace-key forwarding row) moved down by one line (394 and 427:44) and are unchanged otherwise. No record kind, key constructor, forwarding site or route family was added: the producer (`core/setup-agent/eval-environment-source.ts`) reads the company's existing `SAVED_ENV#` and approved test environment rows through their own readers (`documentSavedEnvStore.get`, `readApprovedTestEnvironment`), keyed by the run's own company and project.
